diff: `tmp/units/a1.diff` (baseline `825c464` to `ffbc6e3`, campaign folder\n42\t  excluded).\n43\t- The actual status output: `git status --porcelain` is empty at dispatch; the tree equals\n44\t  `ffbc6e3`.\n45\t- The reconciled plan and design reports under `.orkestrel/scaffold/`.\n46\t- The probe record `.orkestrel/scaffold/probe-e2e.md` — supplied as executed evidence for the\n47\t the guide's\n78\t   claims about the advisories match the driven behavior in the probe record, and the\n79\t   dot-stripped fallback paths the guide and pointers name match `pathToStorage`.\n80\t10. in\n82\t    the prose the diff adds, and every new public export is barrel-reachable and documented.\n83\t11. **`host.json` moved minimally.** Exactly the vendored policy digest and the membership {"parentUuid":"5f47f2b9-e8ec-44ad-a498-1595438b0386","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"daac33c3-da6e-4ea3-a2e1-d5ade0696baa","agentId":"ae81414e1a498caeb","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01V5AtbXFrahHL4L4pgkG9a3","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t---\n2\tname: orkestrel-falsify\n3\tdescription: Run an adversarial audit round against work that already looks finished — write the subject as numbered falsifiable claims, dispatch independent auditors instructed to break them rather than confirm them, reconcile their evidence, and rule. Use before accepting a fix round, before a version bump or publication, when green gates are the only evidence a change works, when a defect has recurred across rounds, or whenever a review would otherwise read a diff and agree with it.\n4\t---\n5\t\n6\t# Falsify\n7\t\n8\tGreen gates prove a suite ran. They prove nothing about the claim the work makes. This skill is for\n9\tthe moment when everything passes and the work is still not known to be right.\n10\t\n11\t## Load authority\n12\t\n13\t1. `AGENTS.md`.\n14\t2. `.claude/rules/quality.md`, especially **Falsification** — the laws governing auditor behaviour.\n15\t   This skill prescribes the round; that section prescribes the conduct inside it. Do not restate it.\n16\t3. The governing guide/spec for the subject.\n17\t4. `references/brief.md` before writing a brief; `references/reconcile.md` before ruling.\n18\t\n19\t## When a round is warranted\n20\t\n21\tRun one when the work is finished and the evidence is weak in a specific way:\n22\t\n23\t- gates are green and nothing has attacked the claim;\n24\t- a fix round is about to be accepted, especially one whose author reported success;\n25\t- a version is about to be bumped, packed, or published;\n26\t- the same defect class has now appeared in more than one round;\n27\t- a self-declared \"sound, unchanged, no fix needed\" verdict is load-bearing.\n28\t\n29\tDo not run one for a typo, a mechanical rename, or work whose failure would be immediately visible.\n30\t\n31\tA round also needs something new to attack. When the previous round's claims all held, nothing has\n32\tbeen added or repaired since, and the only motive is that an auditor could still imagine an attack,\n33\tthere is no subject — closing is the correct action and the next subject is the deliverable.\n34\t\n35\tWhen the same class has recurred along one seam to the budget in `.claude/rules/quality.md`\n36\t§ Rounds and verdicts, the next move is that law's strategy switch — the breadth sweep of the\n37\tstream, the ruling, or the dropped moving-target claim — rather than another successor round.\n38\t\n39\t## Write the brief\n40\t\n41\tThe brief is the instrument. A weak brief produces a confirming review no matter which auditor reads\n42\tit. Follow `references/brief.md`. What this skill adds beyond the conduct law:\n43\t\n44\t- Every re-run is a **successor**, not a restatement. It carries what the previous round closed so\n45\t  nothing is re-reported, and it adds claims that attack **the previous round's own rulings**.\n46\t- The brief names what the round **decides**. An auditor that does not know the stakes calibrates to\n47\t  politeness.\n48\t- Unknowns are named as unknowns, with how the auditor reports back on them.\n49\t\n50\tThe claim form itself is the Falsification law's — read it there. The **verdict shape** below is this\n51\tskill's, because `.agents/orchestration.md` assigns it here; everything else about auditor conduct is the law's.\n52\t\n53\t## Evidence, by subject type\n54\t\n55\tThe brief supplies the evidence its subject actually has. Requiring a diff for a subject that has no\n56\tdiff is a rule the brief cannot satisfy.\n57\t\n58\t| subject                                 | required evidence                                                                     |\n59\t| --------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\n60\t| a code change                           | the actual diff and the actual status output; omitting either is a dispatch deviation |\n61\t| a rendered or externally driven surface | the capture portfolio as primary, source as corroboration                             |\n62\t| a policy, design, or process proposal   | the proposal, the canon it must satisfy, and the record of what motivated it          |\n63\t\n64\t**A subject can occupy more than one row; supply every row it occupies.** A ruling whose fixes\n65\talready landed as edits is both a proposal and a code change, and withholding the diff on the grounds\n66\tthat the subject is \"a proposal\" leaves the auditor unable to check whether a fix changed anything\n67\tnobody claimed.\n68\t\n69\t## Run the round\n70\t\n71\t- **Verify every authority the brief references exists in the tree the auditor is rooted in.** A\n72\t  brief that points at a rule file, section, or guide the executor cannot find delivers nothing while\n73\t  looking like authority — and it fails silently, because an auditor does not report a heading it\n74\t  never saw. Check before dispatch; propagate the missing file rather than restating its contents in\n75\t  the brief. This is the reason restatement felt necessary, and it is the wrong cure.\n76\t- Run the **adversarial pass** on one identical brief: a subjective lane and an objective\n77\t  lane, each a fresh subagent with a clean context, blind to each other. Reconcile them yourself.\n78\t  `.agents/orchestration.md` owns lane definitions, engine assignment, and what happens when an\n79\t  engine is dark; do not restate them here.\n80\t- A round run with one lane is a deviation. Record it rather than glossing it. If an engine is\n81\t  unavailable, the remaining engine runs every lane — it never drops one.\n82\t- **Pair every finder with an independent refuter when the round fans out past the subjective and\n83\t  objective lanes.** The\n84\t  refuter receives one slice's findings, never that finder's work, and is briefed to BREAK them\n85\t  rather than to re-audit the subject. It reproduces each stated vector itself and defaults to\n86\t  refuted when uncertain.\n87\t- Refute on any of these grounds, and name which: the vector does not reproduce; the behaviour is\n88\t  correct and documented; it is unreachable through the public API or a documented seam; it asks\n89\t  for new capability rather than naming a defect; it restates a finding an earlier round\n90\t  repaired; or its diagnosis is wrong — then CONFIRM with the correction.\n91\t- Only a survivor earns a fix unit. An unrefuted finding is a hypothesis. The scope grounds,\n92\t  unreachable and new capability, are what keep a round from drifting into a redesign.\n93\t- **Give every auditor the means to run its attacks.** A lens that can only read returns derivations,\n94\t  and a derivation reads exactly like a verdict — it will confirm a claim that one probe would break.\n95\t- **Tell each auditor exactly where a probe may live, and verify that place works before you say it.**\n96\t  A test runner resolves only what its own configuration includes: a probe written outside every\n97\t  configured project is not discovered at all, and the run reports no test files rather than a result.\n98\t  Where the repository provides a probe project, use it and name that project in the brief;\n99\t  `.claude/rules/tests.md` governs what may live there. Where it does not, the reliable form is a file\n100\t  inside the canonical mirrored suite, run by explicit path. Either way the probe is deleted before the\n101\t  auditor returns, and promoted into a permanent test when it proves something worth keeping. Give each\n102\t  concurrent auditor a distinct filename that already satisfies the repository's test naming\n103\t  convention; never invent a prefix to dodge collisions, and never let two auditors claim one path. A\n104\t  probe left in the mirrored suite is discovered and fails a run nobody else caused.\n105\t- **Run auditors concurrently only when their writes cannot collide.** A lens that can execute\n106\t  still writes probes; give each a distinct path and forbid whole-project runs, or serialize the\n107\t  round. **This binds\n108\t  the orchestrator too:** a tree-wide gate run while a round is live sees the auditors' in-flight probes\n109\t  and reports a failure nobody caused. Wait for the round, or scope the command to paths no auditor\n110\t  owns. Never delete another executor's working file to make your own command pass.\n111\t- **Tell a lane when its own engine wrote the half it is auditing, and tell it to attack that half\n112\t  harder.** A fix round reviewed by the engine that wrote it is the case the round exists to avoid,\n113\t  and where the pass cannot avoid it, naming it is what recovers the round. A clean pass on its own\n114\t  engine's work is the least valuable result a lane can return.\n115\t- Supply the evidence the subject type requires, per the table above.\n116\t- Auditors edit no source and spawn nothing. Read-only describes the SUBJECT, never the lane's\n117\t  tools.\n118\t- **Read the lane's allowlist before writing its brief.** A lane with no write tool cannot create\n119\t  a probe; a lane with no exec tool cannot run one or read `git`. Naming either stops the unit on\n120\t  arrival over a detail the allowlist already settled.\n121\t- Where the lane cannot execute, run the probe yourself, record its control and its output, and\n122\t  supply that record as the lane's evidence. The Orchestrator produces, the lane rules. Never\n123\t  widen a lane's tools to fit a brief.\n124\t- Blind reports are **immutable**. Nothing an auditor returns is edited, merged, or revised — by\n125\t  anyone, including the auditor — once it has been returned.\n126\t\n127\t## Verdict shape\n128\t\n129\tEvery auditor in every round returns exactly this, and nothing else. One shape makes rounds\n130\tcomparable; a round that invents its own cannot be read against the last one.\n131\t\n132\t1. **Numbered verdicts**, one per claim, in the brief's order. Exactly one of these values:\n133\t\n134\t   | value           | meaning                                                                                | evidence                                                                                                                                                      |\n135\t   | --------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\n136\t   | `CONFIRMED`     | attacked and it held                                                                   | as the Falsification law requires                                                                                                                             |\n137\t   | `BROKEN`        | falsified                                                                              | as the Falsification law requires — note it says _input, **state, or interleaving**_, so a concurrency claim is falsified by an interleaving, not by an input |\n138\t   | `UNRESOLVED`    | cannot be decided from the evidence available                                          | what would settle it                                                                                                                                          |\n139\t   | `NOT-EVIDENCED` | a claim about a rendered or externally driven surface the supplied capture cannot show | which capture is missing                                                                                                                                      |\n140\t\n141\t   `CONFIRMED` and `BROKEN` defer; only `UNRESOLVED` and `NOT-EVIDENCED` are this skill's, because\n142\t   the law does not name them. `BROKEN` and `UNRESOLVED` are **separate**: a claim nobody could\n143\t   decide has not been falsified, and it cannot supply the fields falsification requires.\n144\t   `NOT-EVIDENCED` is the token the `analyst` and `reviewer` charters already require; it is kept,\n145\t   not re-invented.\n146\t\n147\t2. **Findings fitting no claim**, if any, each substantiated to the same standard as `BROKEN`.\n148\t\n149\t3. **One terminal line**, and only one:\n150\t\n151\t   ```text\n152\t   VERDICT: PASS — <m> of <m> confirmed, no findings outside the claims\n153\t   VERDICT: FAIL — <n> broken, <u> unresolved, <e> not-evidenced, <x> findings outside the claims\n154\t   ```\n155\t\n156\t   **`PASS` requires every one of these to be true**: every claim `CONFIRMED`, nothing `UNRESOLVED`, nothing\n157\t   `NOT-EVIDENCED`, and no substantiated finding outside the claims. A single substantiated finding\n158\t   forces `FAIL` no matter how the numbered claims landed — otherwise a round can report a real\n159\t   defect and still emit the word that authorises the release.\n160\t\n161\tNo process diary. No summary of what was read.\n162\t\n163\t## When a round comes back all-confirmed\n164\t\n165\tThe Falsification law governs this: the brief goes on trial, not the subject. Apply it, and if the\n166\tclaims turn out to have been descriptive, the successor brief is where the sharper ones go.\n167\t\n168\t## Reconcile and rule\n169\t\n170\tFollow `references/reconcile.md`. The obligations that are not delegable:\n171\t\n172\t- **Reproduce every sharp finding yourself** before acting on it. An auditor's finding is a\n173\t  hypothesis until the orchestrator has run it.\n174\t- **Build before you pack.** `npm pack` runs no build, so it ships whatever `dist/` is on disk. An\n175\t  auditor who packs an artifact to inspect it is reading the last build, not the current source, and\n176\t  will report deleted exports as still shipping. Run the package's build first, and say in the brief\n177\t  that the tarball was built from the commit under audit.\n178\t- **Check the subject before acting on a finding, not only the reasoning.** A brief that names attack\n179\t  vectors teaches the lane those vectors matter, and a lane can hand back the brief's own questions as\n180\t  the subject's claims — demanding coverage for a property the subject never documented. Grep the\n181\t  subject for the claim the finding rests on. Where it is not there, the finding is against the brief.\n182\t- **A disagreement between auditors is rarely a tie to average.** It is usually correct answers\n183\t  to different questions. Find the question each one answered.\n184\t- **Bound every finding**: state what is _not_ broken, and why the adjacent behaviour that looks the\n185\t  same is correct. An audit that reports everything is as useless as one that reports nothing.\n186\t- **Bound the fix before briefing it.** Establish what over-correcting would break, and include that\n187\t  in the fix brief as a constraint.\n188\t- Drop, on the record, any finding neither auditor can substantiate.\n189\t\n190\t## Accept, or run it again\n191\t\n192\tThe threshold is **a `PASS` terminal line on a brief whose claims cover what the subject owns** —\n193\tevery numbered claim `CONFIRMED` on evidence, nothing `UNRESOLVED`, nothing `NOT-EVIDENCED`, no\n194\tsubstantiated finding beside them. Never green gates, which prove only that a suite ran.\n195\t\n196\tA round that finds something is a success, not a delay. The alternative is a consumer finding it\n197\tafter publication, when the version number is already spent. But an unsubstantiated attack is not a\n198\tfinding, and the supply of imaginable ones never runs out: a round is not re-run because an auditor\n199\tcan still think of one. A substantiated finding against something no claim names is real and forces\n200\t`FAIL`; an unsubstantiated one is a claim for the successor brief. The escalation law in\n201\t`.claude/rules/quality.md` governs a subject that keeps producing findings at the same seam — after\n202\tenough of them the ruling owed is on the design, not on the next defect.\n203\t\n204\tWhen a fix round follows, its auditor must be an engine that did not write it, and the next round's\n205\tbrief is the successor of this one.\n206\t"}]},"uuid":"a3964065-2ab9-4d3c-bc76-86d4dbc9695e","timestamp":"2026-08-27T02:58:30.041Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"5f47f2b9-e8ec-44ad-a498-1595438b0386","userType":"external","entrypoint":"remote","cwd":"/home/user/scaffold","sessionId":"dc70cba3-3e2a-551f-9e05-1da04bbbf122","version":"2.1.247","gitBranch":"claude/scaffold-proposal-impl-nabmm9"}

Scaffold keeps the one canonical\n37\t-copy, every target carries a small pointer, and a session attaches scaffold beside the\n38\t-repository it works. Researched and drafted on 2026-08-26; the owner rules on it in a\n39\t-scaffold session.\n40\t-\n41\t-## The problem, measured\n42\t-\n43\t-- A Claude Code cloud session that attaches the whole fleet injects every checkout's\n44\t-  memory set into the model context. The session panel on 2026-08-26 read 335.2k tokens\n45\t-  of memory files — 33.5% of a 1M window — across 44 files: eleven repositories times\n46\t-  the four files each root auto-loads.\n47\t-- The per-repository bytes behind that reading, measured in this checkout: `CLAUDE.md`\n48\t-  2,844, `AGENTS.md` 17,950, `.agents/orchestration.md` 63,388, and\n49\t-  `.claude/rules/writing.md` 8,220 — roughly 92 KiB of always-loaded prose per\n50\t-  repository, byte-identical across all of them.\n51\t-- The damage exceeds the visible percentage. A non-fork subagent inherits the full\n52\t-  memory hierarchy, so in that session an agent pinned to a smaller-window model failed\n53\t-  to boot at all — every launch died on \"Prompt is too long\" before doing any work.\n54\t-\n55\t-## The harness facts, grounded\n56\t-\n57\t-Each fact carries its source; \"undocumented\" marks where the documentation is silent and\n58\t-the session must measure.\n59\t-\n60\t-- Claude Code loads `CLAUDE.md` from the working directory and every ancestor at launch,\n61\t-  after managed policy and `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md`; subtree copies load on reads into\n62\t-  those subtrees (see the memory page, code.claude.com/docs/en/memory).\n63\t-- Locally, `--add-dir` directories do **not** load memory by default; the\n64\t-  `CLAUDE_CODE_ADDITIONAL_DIRECTORIES_CLAUDE_MD=1` variable opts in. Cloud multi-repo\n65\t-  loading is undocumented, and the observed cloud behavior loads every attached root\n66\t-  with no documented off switch.\n67\t-- The `claudeMdExcludes` settings key skips memory files by path or glob at any settings\n68\t-  layer (memory page). Whether the cloud harness honors it is undocumented.\n69\t-- An `@path` import inlines content per importing file — deduplication across importers\n70\t-  is undocumented, so assume none — and an import resolving outside the working\n71\t-  directory triggers an approval dialog. A pointer written as an import would therefore\n72\t-  recreate the duplication or stall on approval; a pointer must be plain prose the agent\n73\t-  follows with a Read.\n74\t-- Rules without `paths` frontmatter load at launch like `.claude/CLAUDE.md`; rules with\n75\t-  `paths` frontmatter load when matching files are read (memory page).\n76\t-- `node_modules` is never auto-loaded as memory. A copy there is present on disk, read\n77\t-  on demand, and injected never — the right property for a fallback.\n78\t-- A non-fork subagent receives every level of the memory hierarchy the main session\n79\t-  loads (see the subagents page, code.claude.com/docs/en/sub-agents).\n80\t-\n81\t-## The design\n82\t-\n83\t-- Scaffold's own working tree stays exactly what it is: the canonical `AGENTS.md`,\n84\t-  `CLAUDE.md`, `.agents/`, `.claude/`, `.codex/`, `.cursor/`, and `.mcp.json`. Editing\n85\t-  them here is the whole change process — no propagation, no fleet churn, no drift, and\n86\t-  `scaffold audit` stops policing copies that no longer exist.\n87\t-- Every target carries small pointer files in their place. The pointer says, as prose:\n88\t-  the coding and orchestration authority is `@orkestrel/scaffold`; when a scaffold\n89\t-  checkout sits beside this repository, read `../scaffold/AGENTS.md` and\n90\t-  `../scaffold/.agents/orchestration.md` before working; otherwise read the same files\n91\t-  from `node_modules/@orkestrel/scaffold/dist/host/`. Write the pointer pair as two\n92\t-  small files (`CLAUDE.md` and `AGENTS.md`) rather than a symlink — the documented\n93\t-  `ln -s` trick breaks on Windows checkouts.\n94\t-- The Codex and Cursor bridges already instruct by reference — `developer_instructions`\n95\t-  in `.codex/config.toml` and the `alwaysApply` rule in `.cursor/rules/` both say \"read\n96\t-  `AGENTS.md`, `.agents/orchestration.md`, …\" — so they shrink to the same pointer\n97\t-  paths rather than changing shape.\n98\t-- A session then attaches scaffold beside the repositories it works. Scaffold's memory\n99\t-  loads once; each target contributes a pointer of a few hundred bytes. A whole-fleet\n100\t-  session carries one canonical copy instead of eleven.\n101\t-- Dispatch briefs name `/home/user/scaffold/AGENTS.md` and its siblings as the files an\n102\t-  executor reads before working, which the brief template already parameterizes.\n103\t-\n104\t-## The migration, mechanically\n105\t-\n106\t-The propagation surface is mapped; every pointer below was verified against this\n107\t-checkout on 2026-08-26.\n108\t-\n109\t-1. Split membership. `HOST_PATHS` (`src/core/constants.ts:124-159`) is one flat list\n110\t-   that drives both staging into `dist/host` (`stageHost`,\n111\t-   `src/server/helpers.ts:1382-1534`) and restoration into targets (`Materializer`,\n112\t-   `src/bin/CLI.ts` `audit`/`repair`). Introduce the distinction the `inferGroup`\n113\t-   labels already sketch (`src/core/helpers.ts:216-227`): the instruction members —\n114\t-   `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `.agents/*`, `.claude/agents`, `.claude/rules`,\n115\t-   `.claude/skills`, `.codex/*`, `.cursor/*`, `.mcp.json` — leave the\n116\t-   vendored-into-target set; the tool surface — editor and git dotfiles, oxlint,\n117\t-   oxfmt, prettier ignore, `configs/`, `scripts/`, the policy tests, `LICENSE`,\n118\t-   `guides/guide.md`, `guides/scaffold.md` — stays vendored. Add the pointer files to the vendored set, so `repair` installs and polices them:\n124\t-   the same destinations (`CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md`), new small content, new digests —\n125\t-   `repair` rewrites them in every target as stale entries.\n126\t-3. Sweep the orphans. `repair` writes only missing and stale entries\n130\t-   (`src/server/Materializer.ts:291-314`); a file removed from the inventory is\n131\t-   unmanaged, not deleted. Each target needs a one-time deletion of the superseded\n132\t-   full copies (`.agents/`, `.claude/agents`, `.claude/rules`, `.claude/skills`,\n133\t-   `.codex/`, `.cursor/`, `.mcp.json`) in the same visit that re-pins and repairs.\n134\t-5. Re-scope the policy tests. `tests/setupPolicy.ts` inspects the skill family under\n135\t-   `.agents/skills` (`:1242-1434`) and the provider bridges under `.claude/skills`\n136\t-   (`:1444-1561`); with those trees absent from targets, verify the inspections pass on\n137\t-   absence in a target while still binding in scaffold's own tree, and re-scope them\n138\t-   where they do not.\n139\t-6. Release. The vendored surface moves, so scaffold bumps and publishes on its own\n140\t-   account; every target re-pins, runs `repair`, takes the one-time sweep, and proves\n141\t-   its gates green.\n142\t-\n143\t-## Options ruled\n144\t-\n145\t-- **Scaffold as single host with target pointers** — the preceding design.\n146\t-  Recommended: it removes the multiplier, ends propagation churn, and makes scaffold\n147\t-  the authority in fact.\n148\t-- **`claudeMdExcludes` in session settings** — documented, immediate, no restructuring.\n149\t-  It leaves propagation and drift in place, needs per-environment configuration, and\n150\t-  its cloud behavior is unverified. Every load-bearing mechanism below was verified by the Orchestrator against the\n7\tsource before adoption.\n8\t\n9\t## Recorded deviations and substitutions\n10\t\n11\t- The Codex bench is dark this session (`codex` CLI absent). Objective design lanes ran on Opus 5\n12\t  (per the engine-assignment table) and on Cursor Grok (the user's directed substitution for Sol,\n13\t  weighted below Opus and verified). Implementation units route to the Opus `implementer`.\n14\t- The `probe` MCP server failed to connect, so the `prove` tool is unavailable. Fallback probes per\n15\t  `.claude/rules/tests.md` § Probes stand in, each with its control recorded.\n16\t- The plan departs from `PROPOSAL.md` step 1 on membership: `.claude/agents`, `.codex/agents`,\n17\t  `.codex/config.toml`, `.cursor/mcp.json`, `.cursor/rules`, `.mcp.json`, and\n18\t  `.claude/settings.json` stay vendored. The mechanical reasons: `Materializer.catalog` throws when\n19\t  `.claude/agents/orkestrel.md` is unreadable (`src/server/Materializer.ts:951-954`); with no\n20\t  planned directory member, `audit` can never report a foreign finding and `remove` can never act\n21\t  (`src/server/Materializer.ts:618-654`); `.mcp.json` registers the `probe` server the root\n22\t  contract mandates; and the loading-mechanism decomposition shows these files contribute no bytes\n23\t  to the measured context cost, so removing them buys nothing the proposal's goal names. Each Opus\n24\t  lane reached this independently.\n25\t\n26\t## Agreements adopted from every lane\n27\t\n28\t- `HOST_PATHS` semantics stay \"the set written into a target\"; a new staged-only constant carries\n29\t  the instruction canon. Staging walks both; `stageHost` sorts entries by storage\n30\t  (`src/server/helpers.ts:1464-1465`), so `host.json` and `dist/host` stay byte-identical.\n31\t- The pointer pair ships as content-owned `template`-origin `ContentArtifact` beside\n32\t  `README.md` — never as a vendored member: `stageHost:1454-1460` refuses two vendored paths at one\n33\t  storage name.\n34\t- The pointer's `node_modules` fallback names storage paths: `pathToStorage` strips the dot that\n35\t  opens each segment, so the staged copies sit at `dist/host/AGENTS.md`,\n36\t  `dist/host/agents/orchestration.md`, and `dist/host/claude/rules/`. `PROPOSAL.md:58` is wrong on\n37\t  this and the pointer corrects it.\n38\t- No deletion mechanism ships. `repair` writes only `missing` and `stale`; the superseded copies\n39\t  become unmanaged; the fleet visit performs `git rm -r` and the wave reference documents it.\n40\t- `PROPOSAL.md` is retired at acceptance: deleted from the tree, archived here and in git history.\n41\t\n42\t## Rulings on the disagreements\n43\t\n44\t- **Canon membership** (`CANON_PATHS`): `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `.agents/orchestration.md`,\n45\t  `.agents/skills`, `.agents/templates`, `.agents/transports`, `.claude/rules`, `.claude/skills`.\n46\t  The whole `.agents/` tree leaves (objective lane over subjective): a target holds no partial\n47\t  `.agents/` tree, and the pointer's resolution paragraph covers every dangling reference a\n48\t  scaffold-supplied file makes. `.claude/rules` is forced out with the pointer conversion: the\n49\t  rule-map inspection reddens a target holding rules under a pointer `AGENTS.md` with no rule map.\n50\t  `.agents/skills` and `.claude/skills` leave together: the bridge inspection fails a lone side.\n51\t- **Live overlay**: the fetch filter in `CLI.#host` and the floor-byte retention in `filesToHost`\n52\t  land as a pair. The filter alone would make `filesToHost` return `undefined` on every run\n53\t  (`src/server/helpers.ts:1222`) and silently force the floor — the objective lane's finding,\n54\t  verified.\n55\t- **Superseded advisory** (subjective lane, adopted): `audit` raises a target question naming canon\n56\t  members still present in a target, on the `#setupQuestion` precedent (`src/bin/CLI.ts:1359`) —\n57\t  detection without deletion, self-extinguishing after the sweep.\n58\t- **Policy re-scope**: the vendored skill-family case asserts the relationship against a direct\n59\t  read of the family root (binds where the root exists, passes on absence); the path-population\n60\t  witness swaps from `.claude/rules/names.md` to `.claude/settings.json`; and a scaffold-side\n61\t  mirrored server test asserts the staged inventory carries\n62\t  `agents/skills/orkestrel-falsify/SKILL.md` and `claude/rules/names.md`, closing the\n63\t  scaffold-trees-vanish hole without a repository-identity branch in a vendored file.\n64\t- **ROADMAP**: one sequenced row for the fleet adoption visit (subjective lane over objective; the\n65\t  repository keeps a roadmap and the visit outlives this commit).\n66\t\n67\t## Exit criterion\n68\t\n69\tThe campaign closes when each of these ends implemented, retained, or intentionally excluded on\n70\tevidence:\n71\t\n72\t1. Membership is `HOST_PATHS` plus `CANON_PATHS`; staging carries both; `host.json` is\n73\t   byte-identical to a fresh `stageInventory` without regeneration.\n74\t2. The pointer pair compiles at `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md`, content-owned, written by `new` and\n75\t   repaired as stale, with storage-correct fallbacks and no `@path` import.\n76\t3. The vendored policy suite passes on a post-migration target shape and still binds in scaffold;\n79\t   the scaffold-side inventory test binds the canon family.\n80\t6. `catalog`, `audit`, `repair`, and `overwrite` still act against a generated target.\n81\t7. Guide, README, ROADMAP, and every executed fence agree with what shipped; `PROPOSAL.md` is\n82\t   retired.\n83\t8. The gate chain is green: `format:check`, `lint:check`, `check`, `build`, `test`.\n84\t\n85\tThe release and the per-target visit (re-pin, `repair`, sweep, gates) are the follow-up the wave\n86\treference documents; publishing is the user's decision.\n87\t\n88\t## Routing ledger\n89\t\n90\t| Unit | Subject | Role | Engine |\n91\t| ---- | ------- | ---- | ------ |\n92\t| W1 | Source contract: constants, predicate, templates, compiler, staging, overlay, advisory, and their mirrored tests | `implementer` | Opus 5 (recorded substitution for Sol) |\n93\t| W2 | Fixture and proof shadows: `setupServer`, Materializer and distribution tests, vendored policy re-scope, scaffold-side inventory test | `implementer` | Opus 5 (recorded substitution for Sol) |\n94\t| W3 | Documentation: guide, README, ROADMAP, wave reference, `PROPOSAL.md` retirement | `implementer` | Opus 5 |\n95\t| A1 | Adversarial audit of W1-W3 | `reviewer` + objective lane | Opus 5 + Cursor Grok (user-directed substitution) + `checker` on Sonnet |\n96\t| V1 | Authoritative gates | `verifier` | Sonnet |\n97\t\n98\tOrchestrator-owned: serial integration, the build-and-inventory proof between units, reconciliation\n99\tof the audit, and acceptance.\n100\t"}]},"uuid":"eb92c7e7-8a26-4820-88c5-2f0ebaf089b4","timestamp":"2026-08-27T02:58:43.841Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"b8357d2a-a4ec-40e0-9e25-e025de5d43f4","userType":"external","entrypoint":"remote","cwd":"/home/user/scaffold","sessionId":"dc70cba3-3e2a-551f-9e05-1da04bbbf122","version":"2.1.247","gitBranch":"claude/scaffold-proposal-impl-nabmm9"}

{"parentUuid":"a9f1fb9f-ea9c-4912-b267-e84cde4119c9","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"daac33c3-da6e-4ea3-a2e1-d5ade0696baa","agentId":"ae81414e1a498caeb","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01138YtbDNAhrbnSvEuSkugy","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t---\n2\tdescription: Cursor bridge to the shared coding contract and orchestration contract.\n3\talwaysApply: true\n4\t---\n5\t\n6\t# Cursor bridge\n7\t\n8\tRead `AGENTS.md`, `.agents/orchestration.md`, every applicable `.claude/rules/*.md` file, the\n9\tdispatch-named `.agents/skills` workflow and its required references, and the governing guide or\n10\tspec before acting.\n11\t\n12\tThose files own the engine split, the adversarial pass, the tedious-work ladder, and every routing\n13\trule, and they are not repeated here. This file adds only how a Cursor session invokes them.\n14\t\n15\t## Which posture\n16\t\n17\tA Cursor session takes one of these postures. Establish which before acting.\n18\t\n19\t- **Invoked through the `grok` bridge** — a read-only Executor. Return distilled evidence with\n20\t  `file:line` pointers. Never edit, decide, or accept.\n21\t- **Driven directly by the user** — the Orchestrator for this harness, per the orchestration\n22\t  contract.\n23\t\n24\t## Reaching the other engines\n25\t\n26\t- Reach Opus 5 and Sol only through the `codex` and `claude` MCP servers registered in\n27\t  `.cursor/mcp.json`, which run those CLIs on their own accounts.\n28\t- Never select another provider's model from Cursor's own model list. Cursor offers Opus, Sol, and\n29\t  others, but running them here spends Cursor credits for work an MCP server does for free. Cursor\n30\t  is the Grok bench and nothing else.\n31\t- Approve both servers once per machine: `agent mcp enable codex` and `agent mcp enable claude`.\n32\t- MCP serves short interactive exchanges only. Long work uses the journaled CLI under the bench\n33\t  laws.\n34\t"}]},"uuid":"a52b7cdb-0669-4a1d-8ff1-cae8911f6126","timestamp":"2026-08-27T02:59:21.458Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"a9f1fb9f-ea9c-4912-b267-e84cde4119c9","userType":"external","entrypoint":"remote","cwd":"/home/user/scaffold","sessionId":"dc70cba3-3e2a-551f-9e05-1da04bbbf122","version":"2.1.247","gitBranch":"claude/scaffold-proposal-impl-nabmm9"}

{"parentUuid":"a852d1b8-ebcc-4553-95de-44cbe8f62341","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"daac33c3-da6e-4ea3-a2e1-d5ade0696baa","agentId":"ae81414e1a498caeb","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_018ASoWqEQbmjTCiWv13hsZp","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# @orkestrel/scaffold\n2\t\n3\tCompile a workspace specification into an ordered list of files, compare that list to a real\n4\tdirectory, and write the difference.\n5\t\n6\tEvery `@orkestrel` repository shares one toolchain, one set of agent instructions, and one set of\n7\troot dotfiles. Scaffold ships that shared set as data inside the package and gives it verbs: create\n8\ta workspace from it, report how a workspace differs from it, and write the difference back.\n9\t\n10\tThe set splits by how a repository meets it. The tool surface is vendored: every target receives its\n11\town copy, and the verbs write it and compare it. The instruction canon — the coding and orchestration\n12\tcontracts, the rules, the skills, the templates, and the transport contracts — is published for\n13\treading instead, from a scaffold checkout sitting beside the repository, or from\n14\t`node_modules/@orkestrel/scaffold/dist/host/` in the installed package. Every target carries the\n15\t`AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md` pointers that name where to read it.\n16\t\n17\t## Install\n18\t\n19\t```sh\n20\tnpm install --save-dev @orkestrel/scaffold\n21\t```\n22\t\n23\tThe executable needs Node 22.12 or later. Run it without installing anything:\n24\t\n25\t```sh\n26\tnpx @orkestrel/scaffold --help\n27\t```\n28\t\n29\t## Verbs\n30\t\n31\tAuthority is the verb's: every verb except `audit` writes when it is typed, and no\n32\toption grants a write. Exit codes are `0` clean, `1` drift or failure, and `2` usage error.\n33\t\n34\t`--target <path>` points any verb at another directory; the working directory is the default.\n35\t`--json` replaces the report with one machine-readable value on standard output.\n36\t\n37\t### `new` — scaffold a workspace\n38\t\n39\t```sh\n40\tnpx scaffold new router --src core,server\n41\t```\n42\t\n43\tWrites a complete workspace into `./router`: its manifest, its build configuration, empty barrels\n44\tfor each selected environment, its tests, its documentation, the `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md`\n45\tpointers, and every vendored file. `--app` selects private application environments on an\n46\tindependent axis, and `--deps` names `@orkestrel/*` runtime dependencies, each pinned to the\n47\tregistry's latest release. `--bin` adds the command-line entry, its test, and its scoped build\n48\tconfiguration.\n49\t\n50\t### `audit` — report how a target compares to its plan\n51\t\n52\t```sh\n53\tnpx scaffold audit --groups configs,orchestration\n54\t```\n55\t\n56\tWrites nothing. Reports one row per path that differs, and exits `1` when anything does. Omit\n57\t`--groups` to cover every group.\n58\t\n59\t### `repair` — write back what drifted\n60\t"}]},"uuid":"37174e14-fc51-4a3d-b3e2-87c74d0be7df","timestamp":"2026-08-27T02:59:21.487Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"a852d1b8-ebcc-4553-95de-44cbe8f62341","userType":"external","entrypoint":"remote","cwd":"/home/user/scaffold","sessionId":"dc70cba3-3e2a-551f-9e05-1da04bbbf122","version":"2.1.247","gitBranch":"claude/scaffold-proposal-impl-nabmm9"}

That is the smallest form the registry publishes. The default response limit is\n1412\t`MAX_REGISTRY_BYTES`, and the default call budget is `MAX_TOTAL_REGISTRY_BYTES`. A package that\n1413\tpasses the response limit comes back as a `failed` verdict naming the limit, which is this reader's\n1414\tbound and not a statement about the package.\n1415\t\n1416\tA status that carries no representation — a `204` or a `205` — is a `failed` verdict naming the\n1417\tstatus, never a `found` answer holding no bytes. A genuinely empty file arrives as a `200` and does\n1418\tread as found.\n1419\t\n1420\tStage and swap a set of files yourself:\n1421\t\n1422\t```ts\n1423\timport { WriteTransaction } from '@orkestrel/scaffold/server'\n1424\t\n1425\tconst transaction = new WriteTransaction('./packages/router', ['AGENTS.md'])\n1426\ttry {\n1427\t\ttransaction.write('AGENTS.md', '# Agents\\n')\n1428\t\ttransaction.commit() // ['AGENTS.md']\n1429\t} finally {\n1430\t\ttransaction.discard()\n1431\t}\n1432\t```\n1433\t\n1434\tA transaction owns a private root beside the target on the same volume, so every promotion is a\n1435\trename rather than a copy. A failure part way through commit restores every destination it already\n1436\tpromoted and removes every directory it created. No destination ever receives half-written bytes.\n1437\tIt is not a journal: a process killed between promotions leaves a mixed target.\n1438\tThe transaction binds each directory's location rather than its lifetime, so an ancestor swapped\n1439\tfor another path, a file, a symlink, or nothing is refused, and one deleted and recreated in place\n1440\tmay not be.\n1441\t\n1442\tNarrow a refusal by its code:\n1443\t\n1444\t```ts\n1445\timport { isScaffoldError, ScaffoldError } from '@orkestrel/scaffold'\n1446\t\n1447\ttry {\n1448\t\tthrow new ScaffoldError('TARGET', 'The target carries no readable manifest.')\n1449\t} catch (error) {"}]},"uuid":"d7c05f07-9f9a-49c5-8c16-d429f3cb53db","timestamp":"2026-08-27T02:59:48.402Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"8a686c09-4b85-48d3-88fe-b4405ce1a3dd","userType":"external","entrypoint":"remote","cwd":"/home/user/scaffold","sessionId":"dc70cba3-3e2a-551f-9e05-1da04bbbf122","version":"2.1.247","gitBranch":"claude/scaffold-proposal-impl-nabmm9"}

{"parentUuid":"8a14dbd3-1a57-4d71-9f1c-4a36bc219c91","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"daac33c3-da6e-4ea3-a2e1-d5ade0696baa","agentId":"ae81414e1a498caeb","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01XaPxiupuNhspF2keXNiaV8","type":"tool_result","content":"1350\t\t//\n1351\t\t// Coverage is read per module: `tests/<name>.ts` is covered by\n1352\t\t// `tests/<name>.test.ts` and by nothing else, which is the pairing the vendored\n1353\t\t// policy proof resolves through `stemToPolicyCandidates`. Keying the silence on\n1354\t\t// the presence of any setup proof instead would retire the question on the\n1355\t\t// first proof a maintainer writes and leave every other module uncovered with\n1356\t\t// no further signal.\n1357\t\t//\n1358\t\t// A vendored module is excluded because `repair` restores it in every target,\n1359\t\t// so a question naming one would fire everywhere and name nothing the\n1360\t\t// maintainer owns.\n1361\t\t//\n1362\t\t// Audit alone raises this. A writing verb refuses an advisory whose next step\n1363\t\t// would replace planned configuration, and this one names nothing scaffold\n1364\t\t// plans, so refusing a repair over it would block a write on a gap no write\n1365\t\t// could close.\n1366\t\t#setupQuestion(target: string, blueprint: Blueprint): TargetQuestion | undefined {\n1367\t\t\tconst tests = resolveContainedPath(target, 'tests')\n1368\t\t\tif (tests === undefined) return undefined\n1369\t\t\tconst entries = listFiles(tests).filter((path) => !path.includes('/'))\n1370\t\t\tconst proofs = new Set(entries.filter((path) => path.endsWith('.test.ts')))\n1371\t\t\tconst seeds = new Map(\n1372\t\t\t\tblueprintToTestArtifacts(blueprint).map(({ path, content }) => [path, content.trim()]),\n1373\t\t\t)\n1374\t\t\tconst modules = entries\n1375\t\t\t\t.filter((path) => {\n1376\t\t\t\t\tif (!path.startsWith('setup') || !path.endsWith('.ts')) return false\n1377\t\t\t\t\tif (path.endsWith('.test.ts') || HOST_PATHS.includes(`tests/${path}`)) return false\n1378\t\t\t\t\tif (proofs.has(`${path.slice(0, -'.ts'.length)}.test.ts`)) return false\n1379\t\t\t\t\tif (resolveContainedPath(tests, path) === undefined) return false\n1380\t\t\t\t\tconst content = (readFileText(tests, path) ?? '').trim()\n1381\t\t\t\t\treturn content !== '' && content !== (seeds.get(`tests/${path}`) ?? '')\n1382\t\t\t\t})\n1383\t\t\t\t.sort()\n1384\t\t\tif (modules.length === 0) return undefined\n1385\t\t\tconst single = modules.length === 1\n1386\t\t\tconst named = modules.map((path) => `tests/${path}`).join(', ')\n1387\t\t\tconst remedies = modules\n1388\t\t\t\t.map((path) => `tests/${path.slice(0, -'.ts'.length)}.test.ts`)\n1389\t\t\t\t.join(', ')\n1390\t\t\t// The remedy asks for coverage, which is the fact the preceding filter decides.\n1391\t\t\t// It does not say what the proof asserts: the reading is text against a\n1392\t\t\t// seed, so a module that exports nothing and only registers a hook reaches\n1393\t\t\t// here, and a remedy demanding a proof of exported behavior leaves that\n1394\t\t\t// maintainer a permanent advisory or a proof that measures nothing.\n1395\t\t\tconst remedy = single\n1396\t\t\t\t? `Add ${remedies} to cover it.`\n1397\t\t\t\t: `Add ${remedies}, each covering the module of the same name.`\n1398\t\t\treturn {\n1399\t\t\t\tfield: 'setup',\n1400\t\t\t\tmessage: `The target at ${target} carries ${single ? 'a test setup module' : 'test setup modules'} that no proof covers: ${named}. ${remedy} The proof's subject is behavior only this workspace can assert, so scaffold does not write it.`,\n1401\t\t\t\tblocking: false,\n1402\t\t\t\tgroups: ['tests'],\n1403\t\t\t}\n1404\t\t}\n1405\t\n1406\t\t// Report the canon copies a target carried before the split. Scaffold once\n1407\t\t// vendored these paths and now stages them for reading instead, so a target\n1408\t\t// generated by an earlier release still holds bytes nothing updates.\n1409\t\t//\n1410\t\t// The reading is the target's own filesystem rather than the plan, because the\n1411\t\t// plan is exactly what no longer names these paths. A member that is a\n1412\t\t// directory is reported as the directory: the whole tree goes, and naming each\n1413\t\t// file beneath it would bury the instruction in a list.\n1414\t\t//\n1415\t\t// The pointer pair sits at canon paths and is planned, so the planned document\n1416\t\t// paths are subtracted. Naming one of them would tell a maintainer to delete\n1417\t\t// what the next repair restores.\n1418\t\t//\n1419\t\t// Audit alone raises this, on the `#setupQuestion` precedent. No verb writes or\n1420\t\t// deletes a canon path, so refusing a write over it would block every write on\n1421\t\t// a gap no write could close. The removal is one commit in the target, and the\n1422\t\t// question goes quiet after it.\n1423\t\t#canonQuestion(target: string, blueprint: Blueprint): TargetQuestion | undefined {\n1424\t\t\tconst planned = new Set(blueprintToDocumentArtifacts(blueprint).map(({ path }) => path))\n1425\t\t\tconst superseded = CANON_PATHS.filter((path) => {\n1426\t\t\t\tif (planned.has(path)) return false\n1427\t\t\t\tconst full = resolveContainedPath(target, path)\n1428\t\t\t\treturn full !== undefined && (isPhysicalFile(full) || isPhysicalDirectory(full))\n1429\t\t\t})\n1430\t\t\tif (superseded.length === 0) return undefined\n1431\t\t\tconst single = superseded.length === 1\n1432\t\t\treturn {\n1433\t\t\t\tfield: 'canon',\n1434\t\t\t\tmessage: `The target at ${target} carries ${single ? 'a superseded instruction path' : 'superseded instruction paths'} the installed package now supplies: ${superseded.join(', ')}. Remove ${single ? 'it' : 'them'} in one commit. 'it' : 'them'}, and the AGENTS.md pointer in this target names where each contract is read instead.`,\n1435\t\t\t\tblocking: false,\n1436\t\t\t\tgroups: ['docs', 'orchestration'],\n1437\t\t\t}\n1438\t\t}\n1439\t\n1440\t\t// Collect the target questions in a fixed order so audit reports every\n1441\t\t// independent advisory and writing verbs refuse the ones a write would act on.\n1442\t\t#targetQuestions(\n1443\t\t\ttarget: string,\n1444\t\t\tblueprint: Blueprint,\n1445\t\t\tgroups: readonly Group[] | undefined,\n1446\t\t\twriting = false,\n1447\t\t): readonly Question[] {\n1448\t\t\tconst questions: TargetQuestion[] = []\n1449\t\t\tif (!writing) {\n1450\t\t\t\tconst script = this.#scriptQuestion(target, blueprint)\n1451\t\t\t\tif (script !== undefined) questions.push(script)\n1452\t\t\t}\n1453\t\t\tconst project = this.#projectQuestion(target, blueprint, writing)\n1454\t\t\tif (project !== undefined) questions.push(project)\n1455\t\t\tconst dependency = this.#dependencyQuestion(target, blueprint, writing)\n1456\t\t\tif (dependency !== undefined) questions.push(dependency)\n1457\t\t\tif (!writing) {\n1458\t\t\t\tconst setup = this.#setupQuestion(target, blueprint)\n1459\t\t\t\tif (setup !== undefined) questions.push(setup)\n1460\t\t\t\tconst canon = this.#canonQuestion(target, blueprint)\n1461\t\t\t\tif (canon !== undefined) questions.push(canon)\n1462\t\t\t}\n1463\t\t\treturn questions\n1464\t\t\t\t.filter(\n1465\t\t\t\t\t(question) =>\n1466\t\t\t\t\t\tgroups === undefined || question.groups.some((group) => groups.includes(group)),\n1467\t\t\t\t)\n1468\t\t\t\t.map(({ field, message, blocking, candidates }): Question => ({\n1469\t\t\t\t\tfield,\n1470\t\t\t\t\tmessage,\n1471\t\t\t\t\tblocking,\n1472\t\t\t\t\t...(candidates === undefined ? {"parentUuid":"4e663dae-0831-4b44-98e6-6ca95980504e","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"daac33c3-da6e-4ea3-a2e1-d5ade0696baa","agentId":"ae81414e1a498caeb","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_014pdr3gdduYeR9WdCjCMYWP","type":"tool_result","content":"213\t */\n214\texport function isCanonPath(path: string): boolean {\n215\t\treturn CANON_PATHS.some((canon) => path === canon || path.startsWith(`${canon}/`))\n216\t}\n217\t\n218\t/**\n219\t * Infer the {@link Group} a path belongs to.\n220\t *\n221\t * @param path - The target-relative path to classify.\n222\t * @returns The group that owns the path.\n223\t *\n224\t * @remarks\n225\t * A path is grouped by what it governs rather than by where it sits. The\n226\t * manifest files are named exactly; anything\n227\t * {@link matchesOrchestrationPath} accepts is orchestration; `src` and `app`\n228\t * are source; `tests`, `guides`, and `docs` carry their own names; the licence\n229\t * and a root Markdown document are docs; and everything else is configuration.\n230\t * A vendored path and a foreign path found in a target are classified here\n231\t * alike, so the plan and the audit never disagree about what a path is.\n232\t *\n233\t * @example\n234\t * ```ts\n235\t * import { inferGroup } from '@orkestrel/scaffold'\n236\t *\n237\t * inferGroup('src/core/index.ts') // 'source'\n238\t * inferGroup('AGENTS.md') // 'docs'\n239\t * inferGroup('.editorconfig') // 'configs'\n240\t * ```\n241\t */\n242\texport function inferGroup(path: string): Group {\n243\t\tif (path === 'package.json' || path === 'package-lock.json') return 'manifest'\n244\t\tif (matchesOrchestrationPath(path)) return 'orchestration'\n245\t\tif (path.startsWith('src/') || path.startsWith('app/')) return 'source'\n246\t\tif (path.startsWith('tests/')) return 'tests'\n247\t\tif (path.startsWith('guides/')) return 'guides'\n248\t\tif (path.startsWith('docs/')) return 'docs'\n249\t\t// The licence and the root instruction documents are the workspace's own prose.\n250\t\tif (path === 'LICENSE') return 'docs'\n251\t\tif (!path.includes('/') && path.endsWith('.md')) return 'docs'\n252\t\treturn 'configs'\n253\t}\n254\t\n255\t/**\n256\t * Serialize one string as a single-quoted TypeScript literal.\n257\t *\n258\t * @param value - The string to serialize.\n259\t * @returns A complete single-quoted literal with line-breaking and delimiter\n260\t * characters escaped.\n261\t *\n262\t * @remarks\n263\t * Configuration templates insert blueprint-derived strings into TypeScript.\n264\t * Keeping this one serializer at that boundary prevents a name from becoming\n265\t * syntax and preserves oxfmt's configured single-quote fixed point.\n266\t *\n267\t * @example"}]},"uuid":"1a9c127b-d058-45fe-b1cf-8760ef8ad972","timestamp":"2026-08-27T03:00:06.536Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"4e663dae-0831-4b44-98e6-6ca95980504e","userType":"external","entrypoint":"remote","cwd":"/home/user/scaffold","sessionId":"dc70cba3-3e2a-551f-9e05-1da04bbbf122","version":"2.1.247","gitBranch":"claude/scaffold-proposal-impl-nabmm9"}

So every dot that opens a\n203- * segment comes off, and a dotted file at the root moves under `dotfiles/` to\n204- * keep it from colliding with an undotted sibling of the same name. The mapping\n205- * is one direction only: a staged host's manifest records the destination each\n206- * storage name answers for, so the reader never re-derives this.\n207- *\n208- * @example\n209- * ```ts\n210- * import { pathToStorage } from '@orkestrel/scaffold/server'\n211- *\n212- * pathToStorage('.gitignore') // 'dotfiles/gitignore'\n213- * pathToStorage('.claude/rules/names.md') // 'claude/rules/names.md'\n214- * pathToStorage('AGENTS.md') // 'AGENTS.md'\n215- * ```\n216- */\n217:export function pathToStorage(path: string): string {\n218-\tconst segments = path.split('/')\n219-\tif (segments.length === 1) {\n220-\t\treturn path.startsWith('.') ? `dotfiles/${path.slice(1)}` : path\n221-\t}\n222-\treturn segments.map((segment) => (segment.startsWith('.') ? segment.slice(1) : segment)).join('/')\n223-}\n224-\n225-/**\n226- * Compute the SHA-256 digest of text.\n227- *\n228- * @param content - The text to digest.\n229- * @returns Sixty-four lowercase hexadecimal digits.\n230- *\n231- * @remarks\n232- * The identity the server face states, and the reason it differs from core's:\n233- * core settled on a folded 64-bit identity because compilation is synchronous by\n234- * contract and the only cryptographic digest a host-independent scope reaches is\n235- * asynchronous. A Node host reaches the real one synchronously, and\n236- * `HostManifest.digest` is documented as SHA-256, so this is what the server\n237- * uses everywhere a digest is claimed."}]},"uuid":"5b291c54-4942-499a-920f-cc1b94cb2de6","timestamp":"2026-08-27T03:00:41.900Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"aa6e43cb-c741-4bc7-9096-cb2d815036ca","userType":"external","entrypoint":"remote","cwd":"/home/user/scaffold","sessionId":"dc70cba3-3e2a-551f-9e05-1da04bbbf122","version":"2.1.247","gitBranch":"claude/scaffold-proposal-impl-nabmm9"}

{"parentUuid":"645ead7d-7ee8-4378-98d2-9eb83edf0699","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"daac33c3-da6e-4ea3-a2e1-d5ade0696baa","agentId":"ae81414e1a498caeb","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_014AM4i7AViFcZYfHi6F6GkM","type":"tool_result","content":"108\texport const BIN_ENTRY_PATH = 'src/bin/main.ts'\n109\t\n110\t/**\n111\t * The paths a target receives from the vendored data root, frozen.\n112\t *\n113\t * @remarks\n114\t * These are the files the fleet shares verbatim and every target holds a copy\n115\t * of: the licence, the harness directories, the session hook scripts, the shared\n116\t * policy register, the byte-identical root dotfiles, and the guide mirrors a\n117\t * generated workspace starts from. A directory entry vendors everything beneath\n118\t * it.\n119\t *\n120\t * A plan carries the subset its target selects, which is why the list is a\n121\t * candidate set rather than a plan: a workspace never mirrors its own guide.\n122\t *\n123\t * The instruction canon is not here. {@link CANON_PATHS} carries it, and a\n124\t * target reads it from the installed package instead of holding a copy.\n125\t */\n126\texport const HOST_PATHS: readonly string[] = Object.freeze([\n127\t\t'LICENSE',\n128\t\t'.claude/agents',\n129\t\t'.claude/settings.json',\n130\t\t'.codex/agents',\n131\t\t'.codex/config.toml',\n132\t\t'.cursor/mcp.json',\n133\t\t'.cursor/rules',\n134\t\t'.mcp.json',\n135\t\t'scripts/deps.sh',\n136\t\t'scripts/cursor.sh',\n137\t\t'scripts/codex.sh',\n138\t\t'scripts/ollama.sh',\n139\t\t'tests/setupPolicy.ts',\n140\t\t'tests/policy.test.ts',\n141\t\t'tests/config.test.ts',\n142\t\t'configs/helpers.ts',\n143\t\t'configs/policy.ts',\n144\t\t'.editorconfig',\n145\t\t'.gitattributes',\n146\t\t'.gitignore',\n147\t\t'.oxfmtrc.json',\n148\t\t'.oxlintrc.json',\n149\t\t'.oxlintignore',\n150\t\t'.prettierignore',\n151\t\t'guides/guide.md',\n152\t\t'guides/scaffold.md',\n153\t])\n154\t\n155\t/**\n156\t * The instruction-canon paths staged for reading rather than for a target, frozen.\n157\t *\n158\t * @remarks\n159\t * The root instruction documents, the orchestration contract every harness\n160\t * bridge points at, the rule map's rules, the skills, the templates, and the\n161\t * transport contracts. A directory entry covers everything beneath it.\n162\t *\n163\t * Staging walks these beside {@link HOST_PATHS}, so a release ships them and a\n164\t * reader reaches them two ways: a scaffold checkout sitting beside the\n165\t * repository, or the `node_modules/@orkestrel/scaffold/dist/host/` root inside\n166\t * the installed package. No plan claims one, no target receives one, and the\n167\t * `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md` pointers scaffold plans are what name those two\n168\t * locations.\n169\t *\n170\t * The two sets are disjoint. The instruction canon — the coding and orchestration\nguides/scaffold.md:15:That root stages the tool surface and the instruction canon, and a target meets them differently.\nguides/scaffold.md:16:`HOST_PATHS` names the tool surface: every target receives a copy, and the verbs write it and compare\nguides/scaffold.md:17:it. `CANON_PATHS` names the instruction canon — the coding and orchestration contracts, the rules,\nguides/scaffold.md:216:| `isCanonPath`               | function | Test whether a path belongs to the instruction canon a target reads.          |\nguides/scaffold.md:689:supplies. {"parentUuid":"a714f94a-1727-47e0-bd8b-b076711ccc29","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"daac33c3-da6e-4ea3-a2e1-d5ade0696baa","agentId":"ae81414e1a498caeb","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01Bs6xN6sB3kfeVDzGMBykGY","type":"tool_result","content":"648\tDependency floors describes rather than to this question. A present section that is not an object\n649\tproduces a question instead of a crash. This question belongs to `configs` and `tests`. `audit`\n650\treports it only when its selection includes either group, without changing its exit semantics.\n651\t`repair` and `overwrite` refuse before writing a selected `configs` or `tests` group. A selection\n652\tthat excludes those groups proceeds, and no verb adds the declaration for you: `package.json` is\n653\tbirth-owned, and the range and script regions are the only parts of it a verb rewrites.\n654\t\n655\t`audit` reports further non-blocking questions, on the `setup` field and on the `canon` field.\n656\t\n657\tThe `setup` question fires when the target carries a filled root `tests/setup*.ts` module that is\n658\tneither a proof itself nor one of the vendored modules every target receives, while no proof of the\n659\tsame stem covers it. A module counts as filled when its text differs from the seed this blueprint\n660\tplans at that same path.\n661\t\n662\tThe comparison reads the module and the seed trimmed, so surrounding whitespace decides nothing: a\n663\ttrailing newline is not authorship, and a module holding whitespace alone reads as empty rather than\n664\tas filled. It is seed-relative rather than a test for emptiness, because the seeds differ by path:\n665\t`tests/setup.ts` is seeded with the empty string and `tests/setupGlobal.ts` is seeded with a `setup`\n666\tfunction body. A test for emptiness therefore raises the question against a freshly materialized\n667\tworkspace. Holding each module to the seed the same blueprint plans at its own path reports what a\n668\tmaintainer wrote rather than what scaffold seeded.\n669\t\n670\tThat reading carries a release-skew limit. A seeded setup module is birth-owned, so `repair` reports\n671\tit aligned and never rewrites it. A target keeps the seed of the release that materialized it. When\n672\ta release moves a planned seed, scaffold raises the question on every target materialized before it,\n673\tagainst a module scaffold wrote and no maintainer touched. `audit` compares each setup module only\n674\twith the seed the installed release plans, and it retains no earlier seed bytes.\n675\t`tests/setupGlobal.ts` is the module that can meet it, because it is the one seeded with more than\n676\tthe empty string. A maintainer meeting that question closes it by writing the proof it asks for, or\n677\tby taking the seed the installed release plans.\n678\t\n679\tCoverage is read per module: `tests/<name>.ts` is covered by `tests/<name>.test.ts` and by nothing\n680\telse, which is the pairing the vendored policy proof resolves. Writing one proof retires that module\n681\tand leaves every other uncovered module named, and the message pairs each module it names with the\n682\tproof that module wants. The question belongs to the `tests` group, so a scoped audit that excludes\n683\t`tests` omits it. Scaffold does not write the proof it asks for, and the question never refuses a\n684\twrite: a writing verb reports it in the terminal audit it prints, because refusing `repair` over a\n685\tgap no write can close would block every write. Run across a fleet, the question is the list of\n686\tpackages carrying a filled setup module that no proof covers.\n687\t\n688\tThe `canon` question fires when the target still holds an instruction path the installed package\n689\tsupplies. Scaffold vendored those paths before the canon split and stages them for reading instead,\n690\tso a target generated before that release carries bytes nothing updates. The reading is the target's\n691\town filesystem rather than the plan, because the plan is exactly what no longer names those paths.\n692\tThe message names each `CANON_PATHS` member the target holds, reported as the directory where the\n693\tmember is a directory, because the whole tree goes and naming every file beneath it would bury the\n694\tinstruction in a list.\n695\t\n696\tThe planned document paths are subtracted. The `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md` pointers sit at canon\n697\tpaths and `repair` restores them, so naming one would tell a maintainer to delete what the next write\n698\tputs back. The remedy is one commit in the target that deletes the named paths, and the question goes\n699\tquiet after it. No verb writes or deletes a canon path, so refusing `repair` over this question would\n700\tblock every write on a gap no write can close. The question belongs to the `docs` and `orchestration`\n701\tgroups, so a scoped audit that excludes both omits it.\n702\t\n703\t### Exit codes\n704\t\n705\t`0` means the target matched its plan and every step completed. `1` means the target drifted or a\n706\tstep failed. `2` means the command line was not a command. It is a candidate list rather than a plan,\n1170\tbecause a workspace never mirrors its own guide.\n1171\t\n1172\t`CANON_PATHS` is the instruction canon, staged for reading instead: the `AGENTS.md` coding contract,\n1173\tthe `CLAUDE.md` harness bridge, the `.agents/orchestration.md` agent-operation contract, the rules\n1174\tunder `.claude/rules/`, the skills under `.agents/skills/` and `.claude/skills/`, the templates under\n1175\t`.agents/templates/`, and the transport contracts under `.agents/transports/`. A release stages every\n1176\tone of them and no plan claims one, so no target receives a copy. A reader reaches them from a\n1177\tscaffold checkout sitting beside the repository, or from the\n1178\t`node_modules/@orkestrel/scaffold/dist/host/` root inside the installed package, and the `AGENTS.md`\n1179\tpointer scaffold plans into a target names each location.\n1180\t\n1181\t`HOST_PATHS` and `CANON_PATHS` are disjoint. A path in both would be planned into a target and\n1182\twithheld from it at once. `isCanonPath` is the one reading of canon membership, matching a member and\n1183\tanything beneath a member that is a directory, so the compiler, the live overlay, and the\n1184\texecutable's advisory never disagree about what a path is.\n1185\t\n1186\tThe `host.json` file at the repository root is the committed live inventory. Each entry carries the\n1187\tSHA-256 digest of its file content, and the inventory carries a membership digest over its declared\n1188\tpaths and file digests. Run `npm run build:inventory` whenever a vendored byte or path changes; the\n1189\t`config` project refuses a stale inventory. The raw SKILL.md text.\n1055- * @returns The parsed fields and exact scalar source, or `undefined` for an unsupported shape.\n1056- */\n1057-export function parseSkillFrontmatter(content: string): SkillFrontmatter | undefined {\n1058-\tconst lines = content.replaceAll('\\r\\n', '\\n').split('\\n')\n1059-\tconst rawLines = content.split('\\n')\n1060-\tif (lines[0] !== '---') return undefined\n1061-\tconst boundary = lines.indexOf('---', 1)\n1062-\tif (boundary === -1) return undefined\n1063-\tconst keys: string[] = []\n1064-\tlet name: string | undefined\n1065-\tlet description: string | undefined"}]},"uuid":"c7f76182-d452-4711-9908-1d983060dbe1","timestamp":"2026-08-27T03:03:28.711Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"dc5b9cd5-e5bd-421e-8883-e6eabb716c35","userType":"external","entrypoint":"remote","cwd":"/home/user/scaffold","sessionId":"dc70cba3-3e2a-551f-9e05-1da04bbbf122","version":"2.1.247","gitBranch":"claude/scaffold-proposal-impl-nabmm9"}

{"parentUuid":"062fa581-eb4e-4b00-a638-50cc724d1ac7","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"daac33c3-da6e-4ea3-a2e1-d5ade0696baa","agentId":"ae81414e1a498caeb","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01AZFwuAjcVb2NPDs6Rt4Fg8","type":"tool_result","content":"986-\n987-/**\n988- * Resolve an exact-case directory beneath a physical root.\n989- *\n990- * @param root - The physical directory from which resolution starts.\n991- * @param path - The relative directory path to resolve.\n992- * @returns The resolved directory, or `undefined` when a segment is absent or not a directory.\n993- */\n994:export function resolvePolicyDirectory(root: string, path: string): string | undefined {\n995-\tconst normalized = normalizePolicyPath(path)\n996-\tif (normalized === '' || normalized === '.') return root\n997-\tlet current = root\n998-\tfor (const segment of normalized.split('/')) {\n999-\t\tconst entry = readdirSync(current, { withFileTypes: true }).find(\n1000-\t\t\t(candidate) => candidate.name === segment && candidate.isDirectory(),\n1001-\t\t)\n1002-\t\tif (entry === undefined) return undefined\n1003-\t\tcurrent = join(current, entry.name)\n1004-\t}\n1005-\treturn current\n1006-}"}]},"uuid":"6f83febb-16ca-4d5c-b5ff-f658563f4f63","timestamp":"2026-08-27T03:03:51.873Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"062fa581-eb4e-4b00-a638-50cc724d1ac7","userType":"external","entrypoint":"remote","cwd":"/home/user/scaffold","sessionId":"dc70cba3-3e2a-551f-9e05-1da04bbbf122","version":"2.1.247","gitBranch":"claude/scaffold-proposal-impl-nabmm9"}

{"parentUuid":"02a60ce8-a47c-41bc-bc02-a472bec35c4c","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"ae81414e1a498caeb","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011CeSWbJz448T4f76LHHsSy","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_014o5d8ncXDWz8Ca9amPBHrY","name":"Read","input":{"file_path":"/home/user/scaffold/.agents/skills/orkestrel-publish/references/wave.md"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1853,"cache_read_input_tokens":169302,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":1853,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":2,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"diagnostics":null},"requestId":"req_011CeSWbJCRnqjA7g84zNNpE","attributionAgent":"reviewer","type":"assistant","uuid":"b2ed565d-750c-4b00-859f-9ecfd31525d7","timestamp":"2026-08-27T03:04:17.903Z","effort":"high","userType":"external","entrypoint":"remote","cwd":"/home/user/scaffold","sessionId":"dc70cba3-3e2a-551f-9e05-1da04bbbf122","version":"2.1.247","gitBranch":"claude/scaffold-proposal-impl-nabmm9"}
{"parentUuid":"b2ed565d-750c-4b00-859f-9ecfd31525d7","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"daac33c3-da6e-4ea3-a2e1-d5ade0696baa","agentId":"ae81414e1a498caeb","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_014o5d8ncXDWz8Ca9amPBHrY","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# The release wave\n2\t\n3\tClose a fleet-wide goal of every package on registry-served versions as a release wave in layer\n4\torder: visit every\n5\trepository once per round with one procedure, publish each layer in one window, and only then\n6\tprepare the next.\n7\t\n8\t## Visit a repository\n9\t\n10\tRun the visit in this order. A step that reads generated or installed state is invalid before the\n11\tstep that writes it.\n12\t\n13\t1. Re-pin the target's `@orkestrel/scaffold` devDependency and install, so the overwrite runs the\n14\t   current vendored host.\n15\t2. Run `scaffold overwrite`.\n16\t3. Where the target still carries an instruction-canon path, delete it. `scaffold audit` names each\n17\t   one in its `canon` question; run `git rm -r` over the named paths in this visit, and prove the\n18\t   removal with the paths' absence and a clean `git status`. No scaffold verb deletes those copies,\n19\t   so a visit that skips this step leaves the target holding instructions nothing updates and the\n20\t   question firing on the next audit.\n21\t4. Force-verify every `@orkestrel` range against a registry sweep taken after the previous layer\n22\t   published.\n23\t5. Run the full install.\n24\t6. Run the mutating `format` script to converge generated writes.\n25\t7. Run the quality gates.\n26\t8. Compare the rebuilt `dist/` against the published tarball for material content.\n27\t\n28\tRestore any unpublished tarball the target is holding before the quality gates run, per\n29\t`.agents/orchestration.md` § Fixing a dependency before it publishes. A distribution proof run\n30\tagainst a local tarball proves the local tarball.\n31\t\n32\tRun visits in parallel slices of disjoint repositories, each slice strictly serial inside itself,\n33\treporting per target. Refuse a failed target, name it, repair it, and re-run it alone.\n34\t\n35\t## Rule on the bump\n36\t\n37\tBump on either trigger: the rebuilt dist differs materially from the published tarball, or the\n38\tfinal runtime dependency set differs from the published packument.\n39\t\n40\t- Test the final dependency set against the packument, never \"did my step move a pin\". The\n41\t  `declare` step inside `scaffold overwrite` re-pins before any later check, so the step-local\n42\t  reading reports nothing moved while the manifest surface did.\n43\t- Treat a re-pinned runtime range as published surface. Without the bump a consumer installs\n44\t  duplicate copies of the moved dependency.\n45\t- Compare material content only, as `.agents/orchestration.md` § What a bump obliges defines it,\n46\t  and take the blast radius of a bump from that section rather than deciding it here.\n47\t- Ship a dist built before the version bump wherever the bump edits no emitted byte. Check that\n48\t  per package rather than assuming it: a package that imports its own `package.json` version into\n49\t  published code emits that version, so its pre-bump dist is stale the moment the version moves.\n50\t  Rebuild after the bump there and pack from the rebuilt tree. The `npm publish --ignore-scripts`\n51\t  command skips `prepack`, so that rebuild is the operator's step rather than the publish's.\n52\t\n53\t## Prepare a layer\n54\t\n55\tAn unpublished package's first version is `0.0.1`. Do not bump it before that first publish. The\n56\tregistry has nothing to serve, so there is no version to move away from, and bumping produces a\n57\tpackage whose history starts at a number nothing explains.\n58\t\n59\tPrepare a published package's layer in this order:\n60\t\n61\t1. **Bump from what the registry serves, not from the local manifest.** A repository's `version`\n62\t   field can sit a release behind what was published from another checkout, and bumping that\n63\t   produces a version the registry already holds, which fails on upload after the whole gate chain\n64\t   has run. Read the registry first.\n65\t2. **Re-pin every `@orkestrel` range to what the registry serves, and install.**\n66\t3. **Sweep the self-pins**, per the following section.\n67\t4. **Run each package's own `prepublishOnly` script to green.**\n68\t5. **Commit and push before the window opens.**\n69\t\n70\tPrepare the next layer only after this one is on the registry. A dependent's new pin cannot\n71\tinstall until the version it names exists, so preparation and publication interleave and cannot be\n72\tbatched ahead.\n73\t\n74\tThe window is for uploads. Every gate, build, install, and commit happens outside it, which is\n75\twhat makes `--ignore-scripts` the right flag at publish time: the artifact was already proved, and\n76\tthe flag is what stops the gate chain running a second time inside the five minutes.\n77\t\n78\t## Sweep the self-pins\n79\t\n80\tA package's own version appears in its source and its tests as a literal, and a bump falsifies\n81\tevery one of them. Run this sweep after the re-pin install, not after the manifest edit.\n82\t\n83\t- `grep` the prior version literal across `tests/` and `src/` in the publishing package, and rule\n84\t  on every hit. A canned packument in a fixture and a looked-up version in a CLI suite carry the\n85\t  version with no tripwire comment beside them, so they surface as a red gate after the bump\n86\t  rather than as a planned edit before it.\n87\t- Move a documented tripwire — a golden digest over generated output — in the same change as the\n88\t  version bump. That is what the tripwire is for.\n89\t- Re-take a generated artifact's digest after the install, because the generated bytes can derive\n90\t  from the installed tree rather than from the manifest. An install that picks up a newly\n91\t  published dependency moves such a digest with no manifest edit at all, and reverting the\n92\t  manifest pin does not restore it.\n93\t- Prove the cause of a moved digest before recording it. Patch the generated bytes back to the\n94\t  prior content and reproduce the prior digest: a digest that stays moved after the pin is\n95\t  reverted has a cause the pin does not explain, and a new digest recorded without that control is\n96\t  a value nobody can check.\n97\t\n98\t## Refresh the registry between layers\n99\t\n100\tRefresh the registry evidence between layers and derive each round's pins from it. A pin can only\n101\tname a version the registry already serves, so a dependency shipping in the same window keeps the\n102\tresolvable previous pin and takes its development-only re-pin after the window closes.\n103\t"}]},"uuid":"f6eb6f87-9e63-40ee-83d9-ecf7a5e8b5bc","timestamp":"2026-08-27T03:04:17.910Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"b2ed565d-750c-4b00-859f-9ecfd31525d7","userType":"external","entrypoint":"remote","cwd":"/home/user/scaffold","sessionId":"dc70cba3-3e2a-551f-9e05-1da04bbbf122","version":"2.1.247","gitBranch":"claude/scaffold-proposal-impl-nabmm9"}

{"parentUuid":"e0195981-1f0f-4a67-994d-a1319ad4e9f0","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"daac33c3-da6e-4ea3-a2e1-d5ade0696baa","agentId":"ae81414e1a498caeb","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_018iwZayiR3vmhvBrW63u2kJ","type":"tool_result","content":"1644\t\n1645\t/**\n1646\t * Inspect the discovered rule family against the root instruction file's rule map.\n1647\t *\n1648\t * A workspace with no rule file has no rule map to keep, so the population is empty there.\n1649\t *\n1650\t * @param root - The workspace root to inspect.\n1651\t * @returns Every unregistered rule file, then every row resolving to no file.\n1652\t */\n1653\texport function inspectPolicyRuleMap(root: string): readonly PolicyViolation[] {\n1654\t\tconst directory = resolvePolicyDirectory(root, POLICY_RULE_ROOT)\n1655\t\tif (directory === undefined) return []\n1656\t\tconst rules = readdirSync(directory, { withFileTypes: true })\n1657\t\t\t.filter((entry) => entry.isFile() && entry.name.endsWith('.md'))\n1658\t\t\t.map((entry) => `${POLICY_RULE_ROOT}/${entry.name}`)\n1659\t\t\t.sort()\n1660\t\tif (rules.length === 0) return []\n1661\t\tconst content = isPolicyFile(root, POLICY_RULE_MAP_FILE)\n1662\t\t\t? readFileSync(join(root, POLICY_RULE_MAP_FILE), 'utf8')\n1663\t\t\t: ''\n1664\t\tconst registered = new Set(readPolicyRuleMap(content))\n1665\t\tconst violations: PolicyViolation[] = []\n1666\t\tfor (const rule of rules) {\n1667\t\t\tif (!registered.has(rule)) {\n1668\t\t\t\tviolations.push(createPolicyViolation('rules', rule, 'the rule map names every rule file'))\n1669\t\t\t}\n1670\t\t}\n1671\t\tfor (const path of registered) {\n1672\t\t\tif (!isPolicyFile(root, path)) {\n1673\t\t\t\tviolations.push(\n1674\t\t\t\t\tcreatePolicyViolation('rules', path, 'every rule-map row resolves to a rule file'),\n1675\t\t\t\t)\n1676\t\t\t}\n1677\t\t}\n1678\t\treturn violations\n1679\t}\n1680\t\n1681\t/**"}]},"uuid":"7a30dadd-7245-4d4a-ac27-ec56f278c180","timestamp":"2026-08-27T03:04:33.039Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"e0195981-1f0f-4a67-994d-a1319ad4e9f0","userType":"external","entrypoint":"remote","cwd":"/home/user/scaffold","sessionId":"dc70cba3-3e2a-551f-9e05-1da04bbbf122","version":"2.1.247","gitBranch":"claude/scaffold-proposal-impl-nabmm9"}
